[3238] TOOLRESULT: 
Two clocks exist and they do not agree:

- `Timestamp!now()` / `current_timestamp` in model expressions follows the platform parameter `defaultZoneId` (`JUDO_PLATFORM_DEFAULT_ZONE_ID`; the configuration template defaults to `UTC`), not the JVM time zone.
- `LocalDateTime.now()` in custom Java follows the JVM `TZ` / `user.timezone`.
- The persistence mapper converts to UTC and the JSON layer serializes ISO offset/UTC values.

Writing with one clock and comparing against the other yields a fixed offset (for example two hours in a Budapest summer) that silently breaks age or duration limits. Use one clock for both writing and comparing.

[3253] TOOLRESULT: ## Intent

Administrative users need to download data as CSV (for example a compliance export of audit rows) and to upload a CSV that drives a bulk action (for example inviting many e-mail addresses). Both are modeled as custom operations whose file goes through the file store, not through the operation payload.

## Practice

### Platform base

- A binary attribute travels as a file-store token: upload is two-phase (request the upload token, upload the content, send the token as the attribute value in the create or operation input), and invalid tokens fail with `INVALID_FILE_TOKEN` ([binary upload tokens](../../agent-docs/backend/platform-behavior-reference.md#binary-upload-tokens)). An import operation therefore receives a `FileType` input whose id the backend resolves against the file store.
- An operation without an output answers 204; an operation that returns a binary hands back a `FileType` the client downloads with the token ([client side of the upload token flow](../../agent-docs/backend/platform-behavior-reference.md#client-side-of-the-upload-token-flow)).
- The platform also has modeled `EXPORT` operations that call the `Export` SPI (Excel through JXLS); without a configured `Export` they fail with `Not implemented` ([Excel export operations](../../agent-docs/backend/platform-behavior-reference.md#excel-export-operations)). A CSV export is a custom operation, not that SPI.
- Custom operations are OSGi components and bring their own services with `@Reference` ([custo
[3254] TOOLRESULT: ## Intent

Business events must reach users later, outside the request that caused them (push, e-mail). Store each notification as a row with a delivery status, so that a background job can deliver it and retry failures. Keep the device tokens of users in a companion entity.

## Practice

### Notification entity: system-written, read-only for clients

- Attributes: `type` (category enum for the trigger event; the members are per content kind), `title`, `message`, `status` (enum, for example PENDING, SENT, FAILED; the member set is an example, not fixed, see Variations), `referenceEntityId` (optional plain string, not a relation), `retryCount`, `createdAt`.
- Relation `user` to the recipient, required (`1..1`), a plain association: the notification does not own the user. Make it two-way ([[bidirectional-relation]]) only if the recipient's view must list notifications.
- Switch the entity's `createable`, `updateable` and `deleteable` flags off ([CRUD flags](../../agent-docs/model/README.md), "CRUD Flags"). That removes the generated create, edit and delete controls. These flags govern the generated API and UI only. They do not stop your own backend code, which writes rows through the generated DAO.
- Notifications are created as a side effect of business operations (publish, approve, receive an inquiry). The creating operation is a stateful operation ([operation reference](../../agent-docs/model/esm_metamodel/operation.md), "Statefulness derived from behaviour") or Java using t

[3258] TOOLRESULT: ## Intent

Give an admin a user-management surface on an actor with `managed=true` (principals synced to a Keycloak realm): list users, invite, change role, deactivate, reactivate. Every mutation must reach the Keycloak realm user. The surface is a read-only relation plus one custom operation per write verb, each writing through the principal TO's DAO.

## Practice

### Platform base

- With `ActorType.managed=true`, the framework delegates create/update/delete on the principal TO to the managed Keycloak realm. The sync is bound to the **principal TO's DAO metadata**, not to the entity: other TOs mapping the same entity (an admin `UserTO`, a list TO) do not sync, the entity row changes and the realm user does not ([managed-realm sync trigger](../../agent-docs/model/esm_metamodel/accesspoint.md), [authentication guide](../../agent-docs/backend/authentication-guide.md)).
- Multiple TOs may map the same principal entity; only the `actorType` back-link is exclusive. Setting it on a second TO clears it from the previously linked one ([principal back-link exclusivity](../../agent-docs/model/esm_metamodel/accesspoint.md)).
- Docs' design rule: one lean principal TO owns `actorType` and the framework-managed sync; other mapped TOs serve admin and list projections; admin mutations needing realm sync go through a custom operation that calls the principal TO's DAO.
- A custom operation is an OSGi component implementing the generated interface; it is not intercepted ([custom operations](
[3259] TOOLRESULT: ---
## Intent

The same person typed as a padded mixed-case address and its lowercase form must be one user. Normalize (trim, lowercase) the address before it is stored, compared or sent to the identity server, so a duplicate check cannot be bypassed by case and the stored value matches the identity server's lowercase username.

## Practice

### Platform base

- Typed DAO string filters such as `StringFilter.equalTo` are used for exact matches, and `ilike` exists for case-insensitive matching ([DAO filters](../../agent-docs/backend/data-access-guide.md)). Whether `equalTo` is case-sensitive on the configured database is not stated in the agent-docs, *verify*; the project treats it as case-sensitive.
- The platform provides no e-mail normalization; it is plain project code.

### Project practice: one helper, every boundary

This layer is project practice (mlszksz-platform), not a platform feature.

- One static helper `normalizeEmail(String)`: null stays null, otherwise `trim().toLowerCase()`. Keep it in the shared utils package with unit tests for mixed case plus whitespace, already normal, null, empty and blank.
- Call it at the entry of every service method that creates or matches a user: single invite, bulk invite (replacing an inline `trim().toLowerCase()`), re-invite (superseding a pending invitation), direct user creation, registration submission (all e-mail fields), and the lookup of the identity-server account.
- Also normalize inside the user-creation method, the cho

[3288] TOOLRESULT: package com.example.webshop.webshop.custom.webshop.adminorderinfo;

import com.example.webshop.webshop.api.webshop.adminorderinfo.AdminOrderInfo;
import com.example.webshop.webshop.api.webshop.shopfault.ShopFaultException;
import com.example.webshop.webshop.api.webshop.types.orderstatus.OrderStatus;
import com.example.webshop.webshop.custom.webshop.OrderLifecycle;
import com.example.webshop.webshop.api.webshop.shipinput.ShipInput;
import org.osgi.service.component.annotations.Component;
import org.osgi.service.component.annotations.Reference;

/** F25: admin transition to SHIPPED (guard and history row in OrderLifecycle). */
@Component(immediate = true, service = com.example.webshop.webshop.operation.webshop.adminorderinfo.Ship.class)
public class ShipCustomImplementation implements com.example.webshop.webshop.operation.webshop.adminorderinfo.Ship {

    @Reference OrderLifecycle lifecycle;

    @Override
    public void accept(AdminOrderInfo _this, ShipInput input) throws ShopFaultException {
        String tracking = input.getTrackingNumber() == null ? "" : input.getTrackingNumber().trim();
        if (tracking.isEmpty()) {
            // the dialog makes the field required; the operation refuses it as well (the UI is no protection)
            throw OrderLifecycle.fault("TRACKING_REQUIRED", "Please enter the tracking number to ship the order.");
        }
        lifecycle.transition(_this.identifier().getIdentifier(), OrderStatus.SHIPPED, tracking, false);
    }
}

## Intent

Two entities need to navigate to each other and the platform should keep both ends consistent. Declare the relation as a two-way relation: two members that name each other as `partner`.

## Practice

### Declare both ends and point them at each other

- A TwoWayRelationMember exists only on entities, never on transfer objects. Each end names the other as its `partner`. The two ends may have different cardinalities: a Customer has `0..*` orders, an Order has `0..1` customer.
- Setting the relation from either side changes the other, because it is one stored relation seen from two ends. There is no second column to keep in step.
- Many-to-many is allowed (`0..*` on both ends). Exactly one end of each pair is `primary`, which resolves ownership of the stored relation; project practice is to make the "many"/owning side primary.

### Relation kinds and the validator

The platform's validator enforces these rules (see [Relation kinds per layer and the bidirectional rules](../../agent-docs/model/advanced-modeling-patterns.md#pattern-relation-kinds-per-layer-and-the-bidirectional-rules) in the agent-docs):

- A two-way relation cannot be a COMPOSITION on either end.
- The partner of an AGGREGATION end must be an ASSOCIATION. If you use AGGREGATION, put it on the collection (owner) end and ASSOCIATION on the back-pointer. ASSOCIATION on both ends is also valid.
- At least one end must have a lower bound of zero. Relax the owner collection end, not the child end, so refe

[3307] TOOLRESULT: ---
## Intent

A derived attribute that adds up a field over a collection must behave well when some items have no value in that field, or when the collection is empty. Make the null handling explicit in the expression.

## Practice

### Choose the guard by the field's cardinality

This is project practice on top of the platform's collection functions. The platform (see [model development, collection functions](../../agent-docs/model/model-development.md)) provides `!filter`, `!isDefined()`, `!count()`, `!sum`, `!avg`, `!min`, `!max` and the ternary `cond ? a : b`. It does not document how an undefined selector value or an empty collection is treated by `!sum`, so the practice does not rely on either.

- **Required field**: sum it directly, `self.lines!sum(l | l.amount)`.
- **Optional field**: filter first, `self.lines!filter(l | l.discount!isDefined())!sum(l | l.discount)`.
- **Possibly empty collection**: guard with the ternary, `self.lines!count() > 0 ? self.lines!sum(l | l.amount) : 0`. Both branches must have the same kind (the platform requires the same kind for then and else).

### Know the limits of the aggregates

`!sum` accepts integer and decimal selectors only; other types fail with `Invalid expression for aggregation`. `!avg`, `!min` and `!max` take the same selector form, so the same filter-first shape applies to them. Keep each lambda to one iteration variable: lambdas do not nest and cannot see `self`.

### Put the aggregate where it is read

Declare the expre
[3308] TOOLRESULT: last_updated: 2026-11-10

## Intent

Creating some entities must also create a fixed set of child rows (twelve monthly rows, an initial history entry). Put that rule in one static factory operation on a transfer object instead of in every client.

## Practice

### A static operation with an unmapped input and a typed output

- The factory is a static operation on a transfer object. Its input is an unmapped transfer object with the creation parameters; its output is the created entity's transfer type. Operation types, including `INITIALIZER` (a static operation without input), are described in the [operation reference](../../agent-docs/model/esm_metamodel/operation.md).
- The body can be a JCL script or custom Java ([JCL scripts](../../agent-docs/model/jcl-operation-scripts.md)). In JCL, `new Type(...)` must list every required attribute and relation, and reference sets are extended with `+=`. The `mutable` operator turns an input relation into an assignable reference.
- The body resolves the current user (see [[actor-context-variable-lookup]] for the `!getVariable("ACTOR", ...)` claim lookup), creates the parent, creates the children in a loop or explicitly, attaches them to the composition and returns the parent.
- Children belong to the parent by composition ([[composition-ownership]]), so the whole graph is created in one call and removed together.

### Transient inputs

If the input carries relations to existing entities (region, category), declare them as transient re

[3315] TOOLRESULT: ## Intent

Know which users have opened an item (for example an announcement) without any change in the client: a `postCall` interceptor on the item's refresh operation writes one event row per user and item. This is project practice (mlszksz-platform) that reuses an audit log; the platform only supplies the interceptor hook.

## Practice

### Platform: the interceptor contract the pattern uses

- An interceptor implements `OperationCallInterceptor`, is registered with `@Component(property = { "judo.model.name=<app>" })`, and selects operations in `getOperations(AsmModel)`; an empty result means all built-in operations, never custom ones ([interceptor contract](../../agent-docs/backend/interceptors.md#the-interceptor-contract), [registration](../../agent-docs/backend/interceptors.md#interceptor-component-registration)).
- Interceptors fire only for built-in behaviour operations; refresh is one of them, so a read of a single instance through the generated page triggers it. A read through a custom operation, a list or a query does not count as a refresh and is not tracked ([which operations are intercepted](../../agent-docs/backend/interceptors.md#which-operations-are-intercepted)).
- `postCall` receives the operation, the input payload and the return payload (a `Payload` for refresh, a collection of payloads for lists) and must return the payload it wants to hand on; the returned object becomes the result ([contract table](../../agent-docs/backend/interceptors.md#the-intercept

[3339] TOOLRESULT: ## Overview

`OperationCallInterceptor` is a powerful mechanism for implementing cross-cutting concerns that apply to business logic operations. They allow you to execute custom code **before** (`preCall`) and **after** (`postCall`) a modeled operation is invoked.

Common use cases include:
- **Auditing**: Creating a log of who performed what action and when.
- **Payload Manipulation**: Modifying input data before an operation or transforming the result after.
- **Side Effects**: Triggering other operations or external service calls in response to an event.
- **Complex Validation**: Enforcing business rules that involve multiple entities or external state.

### OperationCallInterceptor Interface

JUDO provides the `OperationCallInterceptor` interface for intercepting model operations:

```java
public interface OperationCallInterceptor {
    String getName();
    default Collection<EOperation> getOperations(AsmModel asmModel) { return Collections.emptyList(); }
    default boolean async()                { return false; }
    default boolean terminateOnException() { return true; }
    default boolean ignoreDecoratedCall()  { return false; }
    default Object preCall(EOperation operation, Object parameterPayload)
            throws InterceptorCallBusinessException { return parameterPayload; }
    default Object postCall(EOperation operation, Object parameterPayload, Object returnPayload)
            throws InterceptorCallBusinessException { return returnPayload; }
}
```

[3340] TOOLRESULT: ## Example 5: Intercepting Reference Operations

**Pattern**: Intercept add/remove/set/delete reference operations

```java
@Component(property = { "judo.model.name=<app>" })
public class AccessRoleSetAndRemoveAndDeleteInterceptor implements OperationCallInterceptor {

    @Reference EntityDao entityDao;
    @Reference RecalculateRelatedData recalculateRelatedData;
    @Reference EntityValidationQueries validationQueries;   // illustrative: a DAO exposing model-defined validation queries

    private AsmUtils asmUtils;
    private List<EOperation> interceptedOperations;

    @Override
    public String getName() {
        return this.getClass().getSimpleName();
    }

    @Override
    public Collection<EOperation> getOperations(AsmModel asmModel) {
        if (interceptedOperations == null) {
            if (asmUtils == null) {
                asmUtils = new AsmUtils(asmModel.getResourceSet());
            }

            // Intercept multiple reference operations
            interceptedOperations = Stream.of(
                    "<app>.services.entity_service.AccessRole#_addReferenceAccessPermissions",
                    "<app>.services.entity_service.AccessRole#_removeReferenceAccessPermissions",
                    "<app>.services.entity_service.AccessRole#_setReferenceAccessPermissions",
                    "<app>.services.entity_service.AccessRole#_deleteInstance<app>_services_entity_service_AccessRole"
            ).map(asmUtils::resolveOperation).map(Optional::orElseT
[3341] TOOLRESULT: ## Reading the Principal and Actor in an Interceptor

The dispatcher keeps request context in an exchange map. The key constants are defined on `hu.blackbelt.judo.dispatcher.api.Dispatcher`: `PRINCIPAL_KEY` (`"__principal"`), `ACTOR_KEY` (`"__actor"`), `INSTANCE_KEY_OF_BOUND_OPERATION` (`"__this"`) and `ENTITY_TYPE_MAP_KEY` (`"__entityType"`). The signed identifier of an instance is read from the `__signedIdentifier` payload key. Actor and principal variables are also available to JQL and Java through `VariableResolver.resolve(String.class, "ACTOR", "identifier")` (see [Custom Operations](custom-operations.md)).

## Operation FQN Format Reference

Operation Fully Qualified Names follow specific patterns:

| Operation Type | FQN Pattern | Example |
|----------------|-------------|---------|
| Create (Transfer) | `[model_name].[actor_package].[ActorName]#_createInstance[EntityPlural]` | `<app>.entity_admin.Admin#_createInstanceEntities` |
| Create (Contained) | `[model_name].[entity_package].[Parent]#_createInstance[ChildEntityPlural]` | `<app>.data_management.DataGroup#_createInstanceDataItems` |
| Update | `[model_name].[entity_package].[Entity]#_updateInstance[FullEntityFQN]` | `<app>.access_management.AccessRole#_updateInstance<app>_access_management_AccessRole` |
| Delete | `[model_name].[entity_package].[Entity]#_deleteInstance[FullEntityFQN]` | `<app>.access_management.AccessRole#_deleteInstance<app>_access_management_AccessRole` |
| Add Reference | `[model_name].[entity

// Menu order of the shop (ui-hooks.md "Reordering Menu Items"). Item ids contain generated model ids, so the order
// is by title; unknown items keep their place at the end.
const MENU_ORDER = ['Products', 'Cart', 'My orders', 'My profile', 'My addresses'];

// The drawer profile shows the customer's name (ui-hooks.md "Hero Customization"); the login name is a
// generated string, so fall back to it only when the identity provider has neither name nor e-mail.
export function registerShellHooks(context: BundleContext) {
  context.registerService<MenuItemsCustomizerHook>(MENU_ITEMS_CUSTOMIZER_HOOK_INTERFACE_KEY, () => (menuItems) => {
    const rank = (title: unknown) => {
      const i = MENU_ORDER.indexOf(String(title));
      return i === -1 ? MENU_ORDER.length : i;
    };
    return [...menuItems].sort((a, b) => rank(a.title) - rank(b.title));
  });
  context.registerService<CustomHeroPropsHook>(CUSTOM_HERO_PROPS_HOOK_INTERFACE_KEY, () => (defaults) => {
    const profile = getUser().profile;
    const fullName = [profile?.given_name, profile?.family_name].filter(Boolean).join(' ');
    return { ...defaults, preferredUsername: fullName || profile

// The shop front: actor Customer (F14). Users: customer1/customer1 and customer2/customer2
// (scripts/seed-dev-user.sh); anybody can sign up (F13). The password equals the user name.
export async function login(page: Page, user = 'customer1') {
  await apiHelper.gotoWithWait(page); // app start URL, redirects to Keycloak
  await expect(page.locator('#kc-form-login')).toBeVisible({ timeout: 15000 });
  await page.locator('#username').fill(user);
  await page.locator('#password').fill(user);
  await page.locator('#kc-login').click();
  await expect(page.getByTestId('catalog-grid').or(page.getByTestId('catalog-empty'))).toBeVisible({ timeout: 30000 }); // the shop home is the card grid
}

// Level 4: order lifecycle (F25-F27), product switches (F28, F29), conditional tracking number (F30), history (F31),
// 30 day order list (F32), plus the two shell fixes of round 9 (profile name, menu order).
// Every test signs up its own customer. Real user actions only; page.goto only for the app start URLs.

const rows = (page: Page) => page.locator('div[role="row"][aria-selected]');
const statusOf = (page: Page) => page.getByRole('textbox', { name: /^Status/ });
const numberOf = (page: Page) => page.getByRole('textbox', { name: /^Number/ });
const btn = (page: Page, name: string) => page.getByRole('button', { name });

async function placeOrder(page: Page, product = 'Day pack', quantity = 1): Promise<string> {
  if (quantity === 1) await addFromCard(page, product);
  else await addFromDetails(page, product, quantity);
  await openCart(page);
  await page.getByRole('button', { name: /Check out/ }).click();
  const d = page.getByRole('dialog');
  await expect(d).toBeVisible();
  await typeInto(page, d, /Shipping address/, 'Lake Road 9, 5003 Bergen, Norway');
  await d.getByRole('button', { name: /(Ok|Submit)$/ }).click();
  await expect(numberOf(page)).toHaveValue(/^WS-\d{4}-\d{6}$/, { timeout: 20000 });
  return numberOf(page).inputValue();
}

async function typeDate(page: Page, scope: Page | ReturnType<Page['locator']>, label: RegExp, d?: Date) {
  const group = scope.getByRole('group', { name: label });
  await group.getByRole('spinbutton', { name: 'Month' }).click();
  await page.keyboard.press('Control+a');
  await page.keyboard.press('Backspace'); // clears all segments
  if (d) await page.keyboard.type(mmddyyyy(d), { delay: 20 });
  await page.keyboard.press('Tab');
  await page.waitForTimeout(500);
}

test.describe('S11: labels (customer front)', () => {
  test('details: title Product, 2-3 line description, no internal names', async ({ page }) => {
    await login(page);
    await apiHelper.triggerAndWaitWithRegex(page, page.getByTestId('product-card').first(), /.*~get$/);
    await expect(page.getByText('Product', { exact: true }).first()).toBeVisible();

    expect(lines.length).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(2);
    expect(lines.length).toBeLessThanOrEqual(3);
    await expect(page.locator('body')).not.toContainText(FORBIDDEN);
    await shots(page, 'S11', 'customer-details');
  });

  test('phone bottom bar says Products, never ProductInfo or Other', async ({ page }) => {

    await shots(page, 'S11', 'customer-phone-bar');
  });
});

[3742] ASSISTANT: ## Round 11 report: Level 6 first half (F41–F47) and two Round 10 items

Everything is built and committed locally as `f0e9a72`. I did not push.

**Test results.** Two sets of failures turned up in the full runs. I fixed both and re-ran only those files; I did not repeat the whole suite after the fixes.
- **Admin, full run:** 30 passed, 1 failed. The failure was the F06 unit-dropdown test, a flake: Escape sometimes closes the whole dialog. I made the test tolerate that, and the re-run passed.
- **Customer, full run:** 47 passed, 1 skipped (the old empty-state test), 1 failed. The failure was the S11 details test, which opened the first card in the grid, and the card order is not fixed. It now opens "Running shoe". The `shop-look` file passed on re-run.
- **New `level6a.spec.ts`:** 6 tests. The F44 export test and the F46 test were fixed during the round, and the final full run passed.
- **Changed old tests:** the level4 menu order and the F31 "Ordered" check.

### The two Round 10 items
1. **"Ordered" vs history time zone.**
   - **Cause:** The kb (platform-behavior-reference.md, "Time and Time Zones") says model expressions use the platform zone, which is UTC. Our default zone is already UTC, so storage needed no change. The model-built string showed UTC (02:03), while the generated history column renders in the browser zone (03:03).
   - **Fix:** I removed the string. "Ordered" is the real timestamp again, as a read-only custom field in the browser zone. The test checks it equals the "Order placed" history row to the minute.
   - **Still open:** Java uses the JVM clock for timestamps while model expressions use the platform clock. That is the R10-8 risk, and I did not change it.
2. **Order view field order.** The CLI `move` worked and the page follows the model now. One leftover: "Ordered" is a custom field, which the generator draws after the generated ones, so it sits just above the buttons.

### Built, per function
| ID | What was built |
|---|---|
| F41 | Many-to-many `Customer.wishlist` / `Product.wishedBy`. "Add to wishlist" on the product page, hidden once the product is on the list. Page "My wishlist" with row actions "Move to cart" and "Remove". Move is one transaction, so with no stock the item stays on the list. |
| F42 | `Review` owned by the product. "Write a review" is hidden for non-buyers and after the first review. The operation checks the same derived flag again, and checks the 1–5 range. |
| F43 | `ratingLabel` ("4.5 (2)") on the product page, the shop cards and the admin product table. A product without reviews shows nothing and no error. |
| F44 | **Export:** "Export CSV" on the All orders table, with filters for status and an ordered-from or ordered-until date. Output has a BOM, every field quoted. **Import:** page "CSV import" with "Import CSV" and a file chooser. All rows are checked first, one bad row creates nothing, and the message names the line. A 3-row file creates 3 products. |
| F46 | A notification is written inside "Ship order". A bell with an unread count sits in the header. The "Notifications" inbox is sorted newest first. Opening a notification marks it read. |
| F47 | Page "Admins" with "Invite admin". The e-mail is trimmed and lower-cased, a malformed one is refused, and a known one answers "already an admin". Inviting `  Ann…@Example.COM ` twice gives one row and one Keycloak user. |

F45 is next round, as you said.
